## Round 16: my picks, also for your veto

## Round 17: pickups for existing shelves, then my picks

The Classical reading-lists thread left six pickups for lane A's shelves (relayed by the coordinator, 2026-10-10). What happened to each:
- **Spurgeon:** added Lectures to My Students (first and second series, 1875-77, IA; the third series, The Art of Illustration, from Gutenberg 42558), The Soul-Winner (Revell, 1895), and from Gutenberg Around the Wicket Gate, Talks to Farmers and Gleanings among the Sheaves. John Ploughman's Talk was not found in a pre-1930 scan or on Gutenberg in this search.
- **Finney:** added his Memoirs (A. S. Barnes, 1876).
- **Perkins, A Reformed Catholike (1597):** already on the shelf inside the 1616 Workes vol. 1; not added twice.
- **Ryle, Thoughts for Young Men:** no pre-1930 separate printing found on IA (only an undated upload and a 1992 Calvary Press reprint); not added.
- **Dabney, Life of Stonewall Jackson:** still left out. The shelf excludes it as a military biography outside divinity, the same rule as his war memorials; say the word and it comes in.
- **Beza, A Learned Treatise of the Plague (1580):** the `theodore-beza` shelf belongs to PR #14's branch, not this lane; left for that thread.

Round 17 closed 2026-10-10 11:20 CDT: ten new shelves, 104 items (14 clean CCEL/Gutenberg, 90 raw IA OCR), plus eight pickups on Spurgeon and Finney. Fourteen title pages were read by eye where OCR garbled or omitted the name, and each is recorded in `_identity_checked`. Two attributions rest on the catalogue, not the title page: Berridge's *Fragment of the True Religion* (1760) and Parker's *Ecce Deus* (1867), both published anonymously. William Gadsby was skipped: too thin for a shelf. Every `--verify --record` run: 0 mismatched. 0 uids minted.

Round 20 closed 2026-10-10 14:10 CDT: twelve new shelves, 138 items (3 clean: 1 CCEL (Hort's St James) and 2 Gutenberg (Legh Richmond); 135 raw IA OCR). CCEL keyed Hort's Christian Ecclesia from a 1914 reprint, so the 1897 first edition is shelved instead. Anonymous title pages were not shelved on catalogue word alone, except the Numerical Bible (F. W. Grant), whose vol. 5 preface he signs. Title pages read by eye where OCR garbled the name are in `_identity_checked`. Every `--verify --record` run: 0 mismatched. 0 uids minted.

Round 23 closed 2026-10-17 17:23 CDT: twelve new shelves, 252 items (10 clean: 4 CCEL and 6 Gutenberg; 242 raw IA OCR). Two CCEL texts were passed over: Manning's Grounds of Faith (CCEL states an 1843 source, but its title page is a ninth edition of 1875 or later) and Swete's Introduction to the Old Testament in Greek (the 1989 Hendrickson reprint); dated scans are shelved instead. Manning's Catholic title pages sign 'Henry Edward, Archbishop of Westminster' with no surname, so that form is in his identity gate.

Round 24 closed 2026-10-17 17:41 CDT: seventeen new shelves, 91 items (14 clean: 9 CCEL and 5 Gutenberg; 77 raw IA OCR). Every item records its translator or editor where the title page names one. Several CCEL texts were passed over because they are post-1929 translations (Peers's Teresa and John of the Cross), a 1997 photographic reprint (de Sales's Treatise), or state a print source they do not carry (Molinos, Hilton's Ladder); dated scans are shelved instead. Theologia Germanica and The Cloud of Unknowing are anonymous, so their identity check is the work's title. Julian of Norwich fills the gap the omnibus plan lists (Warrack, 1901).

Round 25 closed 2026-10-17 18:12 CDT: seventeen new shelves, 116 items (14 clean: 8 CCEL and 6 Gutenberg; 102 raw IA OCR). Every item records its translator or editor where the title page names one; where the name is garbled in the OCR (Sparrow's Boehme, Elphinston's 1767 Bossuet, Eales's 1895 Song of Songs) no translator is claimed and the title page is quoted in `_rights_checked` instead. IA's catalogue dates were wrong for Eales vols III-IV (1896, not 1889), the Law Boehme vols III-IV (1772 and 1781, not 1764) and every Centenary Liguori volume (1886-1897, not 1887); the title-page years are used. Ignatius's Spiritual Exercises fills a 'not yet shelved' row on the reading-lists thread's short-pieces list. No Aquinas or Bonaventure here: the Aquinas thread owns that ground.

## Round 32: Catholic writers in English, the American Social Gospel and New Theology, biblical geographers and Scottish scholars, Hudson Taylor and Oswald Chambers, before 1930 (my picks)

Twenty authors queued in QUEUE-A.json, each built from title pages read for this round. Leo XIII, Mercier, Poulain, Pourrat and Loisy are English translations, with the translator recorded wherever the title page names one; the rest wrote in English. Channing and Theodore Parker were researched and dropped because lane B shelved them first. Adam may veto any shelf.

## Defects to look at
- Six 18th-century Edwards printings OCR at 77-83% (long s); quote from Dwight/Worcester instead.
- Bunyan: CCEL "Miscellaneous Pieces" and Gutenberg 3613 are probably the same pieces twice.
- CCEL serves no text for several titles it lists (Bonar: Follow the Lamb, How Shall I Go to God, Winners of Souls; Ryle: The Two Bears). Held from IA where possible.
- Summa: three treatise-opening questions lack a QUESTION heading in the Gutenberg text; the Supplement is not a row in adler_shelf.json.
- CCEL keys some texts from late reprints, which its DC.Rights line never says; `fetch_shelf.py` now records the `<printSourceInfo>` edition for every CCEL item it checks and notes any from 1930 or later. Found so far on lane A: **Bunyan's Miscellaneous Pieces from the Clarendon Press edition of 1976** (a modern critical edition; moved to `_pending`, Gutenberg 3613 kept), Calvin's Treatise on Relics from a 2008 Prometheus reprint, 26 Owen titles from the Banner of Truth reprints of 1965-68 (of Goold's 1850-53 edition), Wesley's Journal from Moody 1951, Lightfoot's Apostolic Fathers from Baker 1956, Barnes' New Testament Notes from Baker 1949. Also: **CCEL's Owen Glory of Christ (o/owen/glory, held through fetch_sources.py) is a modernised text**, 591 differences from Goold (found by the press thread, PR #15); Goold vol. 1 on the owen shelf carries the faithful text. The old text under a reprint is public domain; whether the reprint added anything is for a person to check before republishing.
- Many IA text layers carry no Greek: the Greek was read as Latin letters (as with Thayer). Measured in round 10: 0 Greek characters in Lightfoot's Galatians and Philippians, four of Westcott's six volumes, Eadie's Ephesians, Pearson and Alford vol. 1. **Fixed where another copy exists** (after the roving review, 2026-10-03): Lightfoot's Galatians and Philippians and Westcott's John vol. 2, Hebrews and Epistles of John now come from scans that keep their Greek (55,788 to 176,106 Greek characters each). Still none: Eadie's Ephesians (no pre-1930 copy on IA has any), Alford vol. 1, Pearson, J. A. Alexander's twelve volumes (Hebrew and Greek both), Barnes' OT Notes. Two Toronto scans were OCR'd entirely as Greek, English included, and were refused for that. The English commentary in the Greekless copies is usable; their Greek would need re-OCR with `grc` as Thayer had.
- The IA date gate trusts the catalogue, and a catalogue can date a reprint by its source: the only Trapp Commentary scan says 1865 and is a 1958 reprint by its own front matter. It was caught by reading the title page, not by the gate; so was a 1977 Klock and Klock reprint of Eadie's Philippians catalogued 1894. Several shelves label a year as "catalogued" where the title page prints none.

## Decisions that are yours
- **CCEL texts keyed from late reprints.** CCEL took these from 20th- and 21st-century reprints, and nobody has read them for added matter: Barnes' New Testament Notes (Baker, 1949, which may carry a later reviser's changes), 26 Owen titles (Banner of Truth, 1965-68, of Goold's edition), Wesley's Journal (Moody, 1951), Lightfoot's Apostolic Fathers (Baker, 1956) and Calvin's Treatise on Relics (Prometheus, 2008). Bunyan's Miscellaneous Pieces (Clarendon critical edition, 1976) is already moved to `_pending`. Separately, CCEL's Owen Glory of Christ is a modernised text (PR #15 found 591 differences from Goold); it is held through `fetch_sources.py`, not a lane A shelf. Keep the clean CCEL texts with the flag, or swap to raw IA OCR of the old editions where one is on the shelf (Owen's Goold volumes are).
- **More CCEL texts keyed from late printings (round 12).** Finney's *Power from on High* is keyed from a 1944 Christian Literature Crusade printing (its notice says the copyright was not renewed); Torrey's *The Person and Work of the Holy Spirit* from a Zondervan printing of 1974. Same choice as above: keep with the flag, or find an older edition.
- **CCEL texts that name no printing date.** CCEL gives no print source, or none with a year, for six round 12 items: F. B. Meyer's three (The Secret of Guidance, Our Daily Homily vol. 2, The Way into the Holiest), Bruce's The Training of the Twelve, and Whyte's Jacob Behmen and Santa Teresa (Oliphant Anderson & Ferrier, undated). All six authors died by 1929, but an undated source can still be a later reprint with added matter. Same choice as the late-reprint items above.
- **Round 14 CCEL items with no dated print source:** Dods' Expositor's Genesis (New York, Armstrong, no year) and How to Become Like Christ (no print source at all). Dods died in 1909, so the text is old; the question is only whether CCEL keyed a later reprint. Same choice as the items above.
- **Round 14 veto points:** Davenport's four Early English Books scans OCR at 81-87%, readable only with effort; keep them as placeholders for a better text, or drop them and keep only the 1906 Election Sermon. A. B. Davidson's Hebrew Grammar and Syntax are kept for their English; their Hebrew is unusable.
- **Bounds' *The Weapon of Prayer* is held.** It came out after his death, edited by H. W. Hodge; the reviewer reports 1931, which this lane has not verified. A 1931 US book is public domain only if its copyright was never renewed. It is in `_pending` on `e-m-bounds_shelf.json`, not fetched; the other six Bounds books stay.
- **Round 12 veto points:** Finney (New School: his views of the will and of revival measures), Bushnell (moral-influence atonement), and McLeod Campbell if he is shelved (deposed in 1831 over universal atonement). Each is on its own shelf, so a veto drops one file.
- **Four round 11 shelves moved to PR #14** (`george-smeaton`, `hugh-martin`, `john-kennedy-dingwall`, `thomas-mccrie`). Both branches had added them. PR #14 reconciled them in 400f0b9, carrying every source id from this branch, so this branch deleted its copies. Their round 11 rows above describe what this lane found; the shelves themselves now live on #14. Martin's Jonah is the third edition of 1880, not an 1889 reissue as first written.
- **A fetcher fix from the PR #14 review waits on you.** It adds bale, browning, cooper, gouge, martin, row, shaw, shields and walker to the common-word surnames, and it flags a CCEL print source with no year ("Sixth Edition") or one naming a reprint ("1889 (Baker reprint)", the case that missed Schaff's Creeds). Lane A read it and it looks sound, but the change to the shared `fetch_shelf.py` was refused by this session's permissions, so it is not applied. Say yes and the CCEL shelves get re-swept with `--verify --record`.
- **A second fetcher fix waits on you (2026-10-10, from lane C via the coordinator).** `fetch_shelf.py` still flags anything published in 1930 or later, but 1930 books became US public domain on 1 January 2026. The fix computes the cutoff as the current year minus 95 (1931 now, 1932 next year) and adds three tests. Like the PR #14 patch, this session's permissions refused the change to the shared fetcher, so it is not applied. Until it is, a 1930 item gets past only through `_rights_checked` after its title page is read.
- **Round 15 veto points:** Adam Clarke (Arminian and Wesleyan; his Commentary is 42 MB of OCR, the largest set on this lane), W. B. Pope (Wesleyan), Wayland's Domestic Slavery (1845, his published exchange with Richard Fuller, kept as a historical document), and Luther's Table-Talk (Hazlitt's version is a selection with its own reputation). Boyce's Abstract is the CCEL text alone (print source 1887); the 1887 IA scan is an alternate to collate against. Luther's Works vol. 3 (1930) is left out only because of the fetcher's old 1930 cutoff: if you approve the cutoff fix, it can come in.
- **Round 16 veto points:** the seven German divines are mid-19th-century university theologians of the mediating school (Dorner, Julius Müller, Luthardt, Christlieb, Kurtz, Stier) and the Swiss Godet: orthodox in the main, but they read Scripture with the critical tools of their day. Stalker died in 1927, so only printings before 1930 are taken. The Dorner System and the Stier Words of the Lord Jesus are mixed sets from several printings.
- **Round 17 veto points:** William Huntington and John Brine (Huntington is often called antinomian; Brine and J. C. Philpot are high or hyper-Calvinist), Henry Drummond (he read evolution into theology), and Joseph Parker's 27-volume People's Bible (39 volumes on one shelf, the second largest set on this lane after Clarke). Christmas Evans's shelf is three biographies by other hands and one book of his own sermons in translation.
- **Round 18 veto points:** Scofield and Gaebelein (dispensationalists; Scofield's Reference Bible itself is left out), A. B. Simpson and A. J. Gordon (divine healing), J. R. Graves (Landmarkism, and seven of his items are 1928 reprints), Augustus H. Strong (ethical monism in his later work) and the size of Simpson (36 items), Pierson (39) and Gaebelein (35). Pink is held to 1917-1923 texts; his best-known later work is not here.
- **Round 19 veto points:** Mahan and Upham (Oberlin perfectionism and Upham's sympathy with Guyon's quietism), Schmucker (the 'American Lutheranism' that revised the Augsburg Confession), Gerhart (Mercersburg), Forsyth and Flint (late-Victorian theology that engages the critics on their ground), and the size of Campbell Morgan (36 items). Moses D. Hoge and Walther are small shelves: little of either survives in print before 1930.
- **Round 20 veto points:** Darby, Kelly, Grant and Newton (four Brethren shelves, 71 items: dispensationalism, and Grant's number symbolism; Newton broke with Darby in 1847 over Christ's humanity), Govett (partial rapture and millennial reward), Jukes (The Second Death teaches universal restitution), Hort (critical-text scholar; his Westcott-Hort Greek New Testament is left out as joint work) and Kitto (geography and history as much as divinity). Fraser of Brea and Colquhoun are Scottish Marrow-tradition divines; Legh Richmond is a small shelf of evangelical tracts.
- **Round 21 veto points:** Bellett, Groves and Tregelles (early Brethren), Burgon (Traditional Text polemic against Westcott and Hort, whose Hort shelf came in round 20), McCosh and Caird (McCosh's accommodation of evolution; Caird's Hegelian philosophy of religion), Pendleton (Landmark Baptist), Baron (Hebrew Christian mission writing on Israel and prophecy), and the size of Jowett (21 items, mostly devotional).
- **Round 22 veto points:** the premillennial and dispensational cluster (Brookes, West, Peters, Erdman, Gray, Blackstone, whose Jesus Is Coming is also a Zionist tract), Dixon (The True and the False spends ten chapters against Roman Catholicism), Blackstone's three books signed only 'W. E. B.', Havergal's three books of verse, and whether Macduff's anonymous books should be shelved on their 'by the author of' chains.
- **Round 23 veto points:** Manning and Faber (Roman Catholic, Manning's pastoral letters defending papal infallibility), Maurice (his Theological Essays cost him his King's College chair over eternal punishment), Driver, Sanday and Swete (higher criticism), Holland and Stanley (Lux Mundi and the Broad Church), Isaac Williams's Tract-era writing, and the size of Manning (34 items) and Vaughan (35).
- **Round 24 veto points:** the Roman Catholic and Quietist mystics as a group (Guyon and Molinos were condemned in their own church; Fénelon's Maxims of the Saints was condemned at Rome in 1699), Eckhart (some propositions condemned in 1329), Tauler's two doubtful attributions (The Following of Christ, the Meditations), the Middle English editions of Rolle (texts that need a glossary to read), and whether the Castaniza-credited Spiritual Conflict belongs on Scupoli's shelf.
- **Round 25 veto points:** the Little Flowers of St Francis (a fourteenth-century collection about him, kept on his shelf), Catherine of Genoa's Life and Doctrine (the Life is about her), Bevan's Matelda (Mechthild's extracts inside Bevan's own narrative), the Catholic devotional works as a group (Liguori's Glories of Mary among them), Boehme (theosophy, outside orthodox Lutheranism), and Graf's 1928 Bridget (US public domain, not UK).
- **Round 26 veto points:** Döllinger (excommunicated in 1871 after rejecting papal infallibility; his later books argue against it), Grou's Hidden Life (an adaptation of his writings), Rickaby's 1929 Rodriguez (US public domain, not UK), the mixed-edition Butler set, and whether the Janus, Quirinus and Britannia Sancta attributions are enough to shelve them.
- **Round 27 veto points:** Inge's and Underhill's late books (all printed before 1930, but both lived past it, so UK copyright runs on), Gore's Jesus of Nazareth (copyright 1929), Benson's Catholic apologetics, von Hügel's Modernist associations, and shelving Philaret on the strength of a catechism whose title page does not name him.
- **Round 28 veto points:** whether Milner's initials-only End of Religious Controversy can be shelved under his name; Fabiola, left out as fiction; Brownson's polemics and The Spirit-Rapper (a novel-form satire, kept because it is on his Works list); the undated printings named above; and Gasquet's Edward VI, written jointly with Edmund Bishop.
- **Round 29 veto points:** Lamennais, who left the Church in 1834 (his later books are shelved alongside the Essay on Indifference); Herrmann's Systematic Theology (1927) and Harnack's Origin of the New Testament (1925), both US public domain but still in UK copyright; Ritschl's Instruction in the Christian Religion, held back because it sits inside A. T. Swing's study of him (Longmans, 1901) and needs Adam's yes; the title pages whose translator is unnamed (Ritschl's Positive Development, edited by Mackintosh and Macaulay; Dupanloup's Ministry of Catechising; Chateaubriand's Martyrs) or garbled past reading (Alzog vol. I, Dupanloup's Future Council, Uhlhorn's Modern Representations), where no translator is claimed; and John Vianney represented by his Thoughts only, since no English sermons or catechisms before 1930 were found.
- **Round 30 veto points:** Anne Catherine Emmerich's books, which are her visions as written down and shaped by Clemens Brentano, shelved under her name as their title pages put it; Pastor's vols 17-18 (1929), the last before 1930, and the Pastor and Janssen title pages, which say "edited" by Antrobus and Kerr or name Mitchell and Christie, not always "translated"; the continuator shelves split off from Meyer (Lünemann, Huther) and Olshausen (Ebrard, Wiesinger); the undated title pages (Hagenbach's Plumptre edition and Reformation, Schürer Division II vols I and III, van Oosterzee's Year of Salvation, Emmerich's Dolorous Passion, Thérèse's Story of a Soul edited by T. N. Taylor); the translators named only as "a Visitandine", "an Irish Carmelite" or not at all (Bougaud, Thérèse, Emmerich, Hagenbach's History of Christian Doctrines); and Lange's Numbers, shelved from an 1899 printing because the first was not found.
- **Round 31 veto points:** Wellhausen, Pfleiderer, Keim and Deissmann are the higher critics, shelved as part of the tradition's record, not as an endorsement; Deissmann (died 1937) and Zahn (died 1933) are US public domain for these pre-1930 printings but still in UK copyright; the early-printed scans with heavy OCR (Arndt's Garden of Paradise 1716, Francke's 1705-1732 books, Zinzendorf's 1740 and 1742 sermons), whose translators are not named; Tersteegen's Life and Character, half of which is a life of him by others; the books with no translator named on the title page (Wellhausen's Sketch, Pfleiderer's Gifford Lectures and Early Christian Conception, Deissmann's Philology and 1929 book, Beck's Biblical Psychology); and Zinzendorf's books whose title pages name only "the Ordinary of the Brethren's Churches", held as pending.
- **Round 32 veto points:** Loisy was the leading Catholic Modernist, excommunicated in 1908, and is shelved as part of the record; Mercier's Manual of Modern Scholastic Philosophy was written with the Louvain professors and is philosophy, which lane B may prefer to own; Leo XIII's encyclical collections name no translator (the official translations); Edward Robinson's Biblical Researches were made with Eli Smith, named on the title page; several 1920s books are US public domain but still in UK copyright (Rickaby's Old Man's Jottings 1925, McGiffert 1924, Gordon 1925, George Adam Smith's Jeremiah); Hudson Taylor's four undated booklets are held as pending because no pre-1930 printing is proved; Oswald Chambers has only two books printed in his lifetime, and My Utmost for His Highest has no pre-1930 scan.
- **Round 33 veto points:** William Booth's In Darkest England was largely ghost-written by W. T. Stead, though it carries Booth's name; Livingstone's Zambesi narrative was written with his brother Charles, and Paton's autobiography was edited by his brother James; McNabb, Jarrett, Thurston, Brengle and Carmichael all lived past 1930, so their pre-1930 books are US public domain but still in UK copyright; several eighteenth-century scans (Coke's sermons among them) have garbled years and heavy OCR noise.
- **Round 34 veto points:** Swedenborg is the founder of a separate church (the New Church), shelved as part of the record, and most of his English title pages name no translator; Elias Hicks's preaching split American Quakers in 1827, and his 1825 sermons were taken down in shorthand by others (one volume shared with Edward Hicks); Grellet's Memoirs, Carvosso's memoir and Charles Wesley's Journal reach us through editors (Seebohm, his son, Jackson); Bramwell's only book is a life of Ann Cutler, and the memoirs of him are held as pending; Rufus M. Jones (died 1948) is US public domain for these pre-1930 books; Bullinger's and Anderson's prophecy books are dispensationalist; Hannah More's Coelebs is a novel, shelved from the 1858 edition because the early editions are anonymous; a few early scans (Fox's 1698 Epistles, Ellwood's 1749 Davideis, Chillingworth's 1638 first edition) carry heavy OCR noise.
- **Round 35 veto points:** two of Bonomelli's catalogue ids are swapped (the shelf follows the title pages); Fleury's Oxford translator is not named and Newman's essay in it is unsigned; Quesnel's translator is not named (the dedications are signed by Richard Russel); Robertson's History of the Christian Church vols V and VI are 1904 printings; Procter's later editions are revised with Frere, Creighton's Church and the Nation was edited by Louise Creighton, and Keane's Onward and Upward was compiled by Maurice Francis Egan; Herbert Vaughan's pastoral letters name him only as "Herbert, Bishop of Salford"; Palmer of Magdalen's Harmony is held as pending (anonymous); Lingard's History of England is an alternate only; Döllinger's Janus and Quirinus stay on the existing Döllinger shelf; lane B may want Mosheim's Commentaries.
- **CCEL's non-commercial request** applies to all 337 CCEL items on lane A's 76 shelves (counted 2026-10-17) (Spurgeon's 70 among them). Each one's DC.Rights line and CCEL's copyright comment are now recorded in its shelf's `_checks`, and every built book carries `redistribute_whole: false` with `ruling: pending (Adam)` until you say otherwise. Rule on it once for all.
- **The Concise Matthew Henry** is an abridgement by an unnamed hand at an unknown date: keep it, or drop it until its source is found.
- **Two Ryle tracts** (Occupy Till I Come, Do You Pray?) moved to `_pending`: the tightened check showed their OCR never names Ryle (the old check passed on the IA identifier printed in the OCR). Look at the title pages.
- Mint uids for the new slugs.
- Six items the identity check refused because their OCR never names the author (listed under `_pending` in each shelf): shelve them after a look by eye, or leave them out.
- *An Exhortation to Peace and Unity*: doubted Bunyan attribution; keep it on his shelf or not.
- Rutherford editions (Letters, two sermon books) sit on the Andrew Bonar shelf as "ed. Bonar": move them to a Rutherford shelf if you prefer.
- `pipeline/fetch_shelf.py`: a title with only one checkable word ("Works, Dwight ed.") is now flagged, not refused, and a shelf's `_identity_checked` {slug: reason} keeps an item confirmed another way. Without this my check would have refused 9 Edwards volumes you already held. Look at `edwards-worcester-04`'s reason.
- `pipeline/fetch_shelf.py` now refuses wrong-book scans and takes an explicit IA text file name; `pipeline/convert_shelf.py` is new. Both want your review before merge.

## Review follow-ups (2026-10-03, about 02:10 CDT)

- **Namesakes.** A name form two shelves share cannot tell their texts apart: bare "hodge" passed Charles Hodge's text on A. A. Hodge's shelf and back again, and both John Browns matched on "john brown". `fetch_shelf.py` now prints a NOTE at load for any form another shelf also claims (never a stop, so no shelf breaks). Lane A's nine affected shelves now use forms that differ: Charles Hodge, A. A. Hodge, John Brown of Edinburgh ("John Brown, D.D."), John Brown of Haddington, William Perkins, Andrew and Horatius Bonar, Ezekiel and Samuel Hopkins. Five items whose title page OCR mangled the name are recorded in `_identity_checked`, with the title-page reading quoted. One limit stays: The Life of Charles Hodge names Charles Hodge on every page, so his name form will always pass it.
- "trench" joined the common-word list; R. C. Trench still matches on his full name.
- **Overlap with PR #11** (verse-keyed commentaries): Westcott on Hebrews and the Epistles of John, Lightfoot on Galatians, Philippians, Colossians and Philemon, and Alford are on lane A shelves as raw OCR too. Each shelf's `_about` says so. You pick which copy is the reading of record. I will not add Ellicott, Bengel or Keil-Delitzsch.
